His struggle against the British joined the boycott of imported goods to the development of Indian industry, the refusal of colonial courts to the establishment of arbitration courts, and the rejection of government education to the founding of national schools and colleges: economic, legal and intellectual independence had to be built together, because the colonial order kept its authority through their conjunction. He also spoke of extending compassion to those who had ruled over his people, because he recognised that a coherent account of reality must include the possibility of becoming the world one has overturned, and of that world's own self-overcoming; sublation, Hegel's *Aufhebung*, which cancels and preserves at once, is after all Spirit's own method.[^s01-aurobindo-compassion] Gebser, driven across Europe by a technically accomplished culture that had turned its achievements to domination and destruction, met the same problem from the other side. Our re-definition of God already implies this inversionary capacity, and what has stumped the monotheistic conceptions of the Abrahamic religions, God's goodness and beauty set against the world's all too apparent capacity for evil and ugliness, we approach as a present challenge: to integrate, and to return to the notion of the whole, its capacity to fall from its own higher ideals. Jung took that step in *Answer to Job*, where the God-image is made to answer for its own darkness, and where Sophia, who shares essential qualities with the Johannine Logos, unmistakably recalls the Indian Shakti; his account of libido as psychic energy carries that feminine power into psychological terms.[^s01-job] We can think of the "consciousness" Gebser sees evolving through time in the same register, holding the axes of conscious and unconscious, collective and personal identity, as one dynamic, evolving force of inter-agentic experiential reality. Histories belong inside the question of consciousness and divinity because they all ask the question of *power*. What people recognise as wealth, legitimate authority or reliable knowledge depends on how they understand the life they share, and that understanding is then taught, rewarded and enforced. A change of ruler leaves much intact when the inherited purposes of rule go unquestioned, so an integral transformation has to reach the economy of value through which a culture recognises its own success, and that economy reaches as far as the value a culture places on consciousness itself: which experiences it credits, which it discounts, and who is entitled to study the difference.

Our reading of the mental-rational achievement is that its third-person power became so generative that it could masquerade as the whole of truth, aspiring to a view from nowhere while the first two persons were discounted as private feeling and subjective noise. Jung's notion of **psychic facticity** refuses that discount: before deciding whether a dream, vision, hunch, sensed presence, mystical unity or extraordinary capacity corresponds to an independently existing object, something happened *in and to a life*, and its occurrence may reorganise the fundamental propositions and beliefs that uphold one's sense of "what's real". Experience, report, symbolic articulation and causal explanation are different evidential acts, so a person can receive the experience in full while its ontology stays open. Sri Aurobindo's *Record of Yoga* shows what a science beginning from this side looks like.[^s01-record-of-yoga] He records siddhis, telepathic impressions, altered states, predictions, failures of exactness, reversals and occasions when inference contaminated perception; whatever we finally judge of his ontological interpretation, the record treats transformations of consciousness as phenomena to be cultivated, discriminated, tested and corrected, and refuses to dismiss them because they arise through a first-person aperture. Such traditions bring a global scientific culture more than exotic "beliefs" to catalogue: they bring trained distinctions within experience that may challenge what the receiving culture thinks an observation is. A genuinely global mind cannot collect Asian, Indigenous, mystical or contemplative knowledge as third-person cultural data while reserving to one historically local epistemology the sole right to determine reality; it has to risk the harder encounter in which another mode of knowing can alter its questions. Gebser's co-presence lets us hold this without abandoning discrimination: magical, mythical, mental-rational and integral capacities need not prove one another for their disclosures to become mutually consequential.

Consciousness research has been governed by every operation of dia-ballein at once. In public, testimony to unusual experience has met cancellation, `(−1)+(+1)=0`: report and reporter are summed to nothing under the names of superstition, irrationality, pathology or error. In private, the institutions that cancelled it studied the malleability of consciousness as something operationally valuable and kept the whole span of what they learned, `(+1)−(−1)=+2`, and entered the subject as its mirror, `(−1)−(+1)=−2`, bearing the cost of the experiment while the agency held its meaning and its yield. CIA funding of Ewen Cameron's work under MKULTRA Subproject 68 supported research into *depatterning* and *psychic driving*: intensive interventions meant to break established psychic patterns, followed by repeated verbal material meant to form new ones.[^s01-mkultra] Its essential image is terrible and, for our purposes, philosophically exact, because depatterning writes the crossed zero into a person: an achieved organisation of self is struck through, and a stroke supplied from outside is driven in to stand where the person's own mediation had stood. When the Senate held its hearings in 1977, it could establish MKULTRA as a programme of research into behavioural modification only from financial records that had survived Richard Helms's order of 1973 to destroy the files, so the archive had itself been depatterned before anyone could read it. In July 2026 a House task force on declassification reopened the matter, hearing Stephen Kinzer on Sidney Gottlieb's programme, on the destroyed records, and on the absence, more than seventy years on, of any prosecution or compensation.[^s01-mkultra-2026] Such research reaches beyond MKULTRA's own dates and borders, and its lineage is often traced back through Operation Paperclip, which brought German scientists, Nazi party members among them, into American programmes after the war. It amounts to an inverted initiation, because sacred and diabolic approaches to the alteration of mind alike recognise that the egoic organisation through which a person calls themselves "me" can be disrupted, and that what follows depends profoundly on the context, authority and intention governing the passage. Traditional initiation tries, at its best, to return a person from such destabilisation with enlarged responsibilities and a wider belonging, the two halves of a life rejoined as `(0/1)/(1/0)`; coercive behavioural manipulation exploits the same plasticity while denying the person sovereignty over the encounter. Humanity, it seems, has been treating "internality" as an "object" for quite a long while, and for rather specific reasons.

Nor did that state keep to the mental structure in which it published its reasons. From 1972 until 1995 American intelligence funded research into remote viewing, first at the Stanford Research Institute and later under the name Stargate, testing as instruments of espionage the very capacities, telepathy and clairvoyance, that Gebser counted among the magical structure.[^s01-stargate] Michael Aquino, then a US Army PSYOP officer, co-wrote with Paul Vallely *From PSYOP to MindWar* (1980), which proposed to carry psychological operations beyond the battlefield and address "friends, enemies, and neutrals" through electronic media, so that attention, interpretation and ambient reality became a field of strategic power: dia-ballein's polar axis drawn across a whole population, with every listener assigned a sign.[^s01-mindwar] Five years earlier Aquino had founded the Temple of Set, a religion of the left-hand path whose god is the Egyptian Set and whose ideal is a consciousness that comes into being apart from the natural order and makes itself divine, an aim our account reads as the part claiming to be its own origin.[^s01-temple-of-set] The co-presence of structures, layered in every life, was here put to work: a mental-rational institution borrowed magical capacities and mythic identities while discounting both in public. Recently released Epstein materials add a contemporary and morally uglier echo, insofar as they document his active cultivation of networks concerned with consciousness, meditation, psi, neuroscience and the engineering of mind, among them a 2017 proposal by the consciousness researcher Jeffery Martin to make Epstein an "empirical experiment": to cultivate capacities for anomalous experience deliberately and engineer experiences meant to open a route toward what Martin called PNSE, persistent non-symbolic experience, encompassing enlightenment and nonduality.[^s01-epstein] These very different histories make visible a **reality asymmetry**: one population's testimony can be culturally disqualified while another institution reserves the right to investigate, classify, weaponise or monetise the conditions from which such testimony arises. Sanity itself is drawn into an economy of frames, always exposed to a culture's power to decide which forms of first-person reality receive a public language and which are translated at once into pathology. A person becomes a case, a score, a diagnosis, a security problem: another determinate `1`. A hidden God, the *Deus absconditus* of the theologians, separates the genuine metaphysical problem from its political counterfeit. The Subject cannot be made answerable as another object because it is the condition through which objects appear, whereas Cameron, an intelligence agency, a corporation, a laboratory or a government is finite, locatable and capable of answering for what it does; when such an institution controls the aperture while withdrawing its own position from scrutiny, it plays the `0`, a finite `1` occupying the place of the condition it can never be. Epstein's co-conspirators are shielded while witness identities are leaked, an exact inversion of the professed purpose of redacting the files, and secrecy becomes authority, occultation a technique of power. Even where little "justice" is served, revelations such as the Epstein files or the WikiLeaks releases bring a palpable recession of trust in the institutions playing the `0`; unfortunately amnesia, the wish of an unbearable `1` to retreat into a `0` condition, often decides the consequences more than the revelations themselves.

Context includes Objective Internality once that layer is unveiled, and so knowledge can no longer be discussed as though nobody held it. Every one of these programmes worked on it: on the remembered experiences, images, rules and habits through which a person meets a world, which can be investigated as objects and are at the same time lived from within as a way of knowing. With that layer in view, a person's reasons for holding a piece of knowledge, and for using it as they do, belong to its context as surely as its evidence does, and the trust relationship that knowledge so often exists to deny comes into the open. Our need to trust arrives together with the fear of uncertainty, and knowledge is readily recruited to deny the uncertainty and, with it, the trust that uncertainty forces on us. Faith lives the dependence through the account's limitation; the defended account instead organises whom we must distrust in order to feel certain. Children have an uncanny capacity to see the real reason behind an adult's expression. When a parent tells a child to "be realistic", the instruction may carry years of work, debt, disappointment and concern for the child's future, and the child may hear beneath the advice the parent's buried sense of not having achieved, of having fallen short of some personal or external expectation and settled. The parent hears themselves offering protection against injuries they know too well; the child hears an old disappointment handed on, like a debt passed down a family, as though no other future could be real. Both are right, and either sentence taken alone leaves most of their disagreement unexplained. Subjective Immediacy, the knower, is distinguishable from these means and knows through them; the world, lived as Worldhood, is what becomes available within their activity; and the means themselves, lived and minded, are what we call Life or Mind. Turned toward at **#4**, that composition becomes accessible from within: something I had attributed wholly to the object, its implausibility, danger or insignificance, can disclose how I was approaching it. What changes is the world I am able to meet, because the conditions through which it appears are themselves becoming intelligible, and so who I am *in* that world, for my Self and for Others, changes decisively. Contextual understanding is invested understanding, because what either party says changes the possibilities open to the other. A depatterning programme attacks this composition from outside and an initiation renews it from within, and they differ in whether the person is returned to their means as their own.

Gebser's structures can now be laid against the QL positions as an anatomy of Objective Internality: each structure is a way the means of knowing are organised, trusted and valued, a household of the mind with its own economy of credit. Set out in the table, the alignment keeps apart three things that have so far been braided together: the **Origin**, the structures through which it becomes manifest, and the recognition of its presence throughout them. Six canonical names, **Ground, Definition, Dynamis, Pattern, Context and Quintessence**, say what each position contributes to the formation of an intelligible world; Gebser's dimensional sequence supplies the cultural reading through the integral, and the supramental horizon developed through Aurobindo concerns the realisation of their unity in knowing and living. Each cultural form in the table persists and combines within later societies, as the layers of a single life persist in an adult. A glyph heads each row as a hook for the eye: `?/!`, query and assertion held in one relation, for the `0/1` from which every determination is made and which opens onto the eight determinations as the full field of glyphs; point, line, angle, triangle, square and circle for the six positions; and the lemniscate for the Möbius return.

| | Glyph | QL position | Gebser structure and dimension | Knowing, value and cultural organisation |
|---|:---:|---|---|---|
| `0/1` | ?/! | Origin in manifestation | Prior to dimensional determination: the formless and its appearing held in one relation. | The metaphysical condition of every structure. `0` names what cannot become one more determinate thing, `1` its actual manifestation, and the slash their belonging. Origin is present through every world a culture inhabits, its ways of distinguishing the real included. |
| **#0** | · | Ground | **Archaic**, zero-dimensional: self and world are not yet separately articulated positions. | Primordial participation, before a differentiated ego claims a world as its object. Human life is implicit within the encompassing reality from which its later distinctions emerge. |
| **#1** | — | Definition | **Magical**, one-dimensional, point-like participation: vital connection precedes ordinary spatial and temporal separation. | In tribal and clan-based ritual life, totem, name, incantation or token participates in the power it invokes. Knowing is attunement and effective participation, and action on the part reaches toward the whole. The first mark is the name through which a power becomes approachable. |
| **#2** | ∟ | Dynamis | **Mythical**, two-dimensional polarity: an inward world of soul, image and rhythmic time. | Oral epics, ancestral narratives, seasonal rites and the sacred dramas of agrarian civilisations make powers intelligible through their actions on one another. Value is recognised through a place in that living drama; the image carries a transformation. |
| **#3** | △ | Pattern | **Mental-rational**, three-dimensional and perspectival: objects and relations articulated from an identifiable position. | Philosophical argument, geometrical representation, written law and scientific explanation make claims available for explicit comparison; industrial, bureaucratic and imperial orders extend these powers through measurement, administration and technical intervention. In its deficient form the authorised model is taken for reality and other ways of knowing are subordinated to its terms. |
| **#4** | □ | Context | **Integral**, four-dimensional and aperspectival: time and the other structures become concretely present within understanding. | Magical participation, mythical significance and rational distinction become intelligible together with the circumstances of their use. Inherited authorities, measures and institutions become examinable as ways of organising a common life; a view discloses its own formation, and the people it governs can take part in revising its terms. |
| **#5** | ⊙ | Quintessence | **Supramental**: dimensional differentiation consciously integrated through its source. | Aurobindo's knowing by identity supplies the horizon: the Many are known through the One whose distinctions they are. In QL, contextual recognition is realised through the activity of a life, with *samāveśa* as the Śaiva language of entry into that unity. |
| `5→0` | ∞ | Möbius return to Origin | The differentiated whole identified with its pre-dimensional condition. | What the traversal recognises is the source present throughout it. The return keeps the history and capacities of differentiation and acknowledges their ground in their exercise, so every further act begins within the same `0/1` with the acquired understanding able to change how it proceeds. |

Vertically, the table follows the development of capacities; read through any present encounter, it shows them acting together. The same agencies reasoned through statistics and budgets while they tested clairvoyance and while their officers took mythic names, and any state gathers allegiance through mythical images and invests its insignia with an almost magical inviolability. An integral understanding makes these activities available to the people taking part in them: to examine the statistical method together with the allegiance directing its use (which often operates unconsciously, beneath the surface presentation of political positions and tenets), including why some consequences seem tolerable when they are attributed to our own side. Placing **Context** after **Pattern** makes a recognised pattern one of the things to understand within a wider event, together with the process by which we recognised it, and so opens a recursive interior in the sixfold: the sources, embodiments, relations and recognitions involved in the present account can themselves be differentiated and investigated, and the context includes the person who supplied the pattern and the value they had invested in finding it. The step has an exact geometry: at **#3** we can set a three-sided polygon, the triangle, whose internal angles sum to 180 degrees; the triangle is the minimum polygon and the basis of tiling and tessellation. At **#4** we move to the four-sided square, and the internal angles *double* to 360 degrees. A square decomposes into two triangles in mutual, diametric co-opposition, and symbolically *context requires at least two perspectives*, and those perspectives have to cover the two main modes of perspectival interpretation, the favouring and the unfavouring chiralities. Relative to a contextual understanding every perspective is one-sided, and it never comes clean or without implication, however much a view from nowhere would claim to stand beyond the given picture: whether consciously or not, a perspective is a certain slice of what matters to someone, and its greater part is always what it cannot see. Apoha, exclusion, is this phantom other portion, everything lying outside the given frame, the gallery and cultural climate that give the picture its worth and intelligibility. Its rejected symmetry, the second triangle, haunts a one-sided perspective as its *shadow*. Traditional Kabbalistic symbolism drew this as the superposition of a triangle and its inverted other half, the star of David, the psyche's bivalent nature as the conjunction of its conscious and unconscious portions. Contextual, integral understanding is therefore shadow-aware and shadow-inclusive, as Jung's reading of the historical necessity of our times concurs.

Latin **textus** meant texture, tissue or an interwoven structure, and was also used for the composition of discourse; **contexere** meant to weave or join together, and *contextus* named the resulting connection or coherence, so that fabric and connected speech already shared one family of words. Weave, texture and lamination point to one another: the threads crossing, the feel of the crossing, and the layers it lays down over time, which is how the mutations settle in a life. The "text" of a life is one's account of it, necessarily authored and held from a given perspective, and a "context" is a weave of many such views. This informs my reading of Gebser's integral stage as requiring an attitude of **being with the text**, a more extensive epistemic task than interpreting propositions. To come closer to an integral understanding we have to follow the threads through which an utterance became possible: what its speaker remembers, what they expect us to understand, what they fear saying or wilfully obscure, and what our answer will permit or prevent. Above all, this *withness of texts* defines an epistemic attitude that seeks understanding by tracing the resonances between ways of seeing, more than by comparing them and judging which one, wholesale, ought to prevail over another. Each view presents itself as definite, and its definiteness belongs to a texture of relations, our own reading among them. Being with the text therefore asks for a withness of the Subject as well: a mind transparent enough to itself, or at least open enough, that its own biases and proclivities can be pointed out when they act, by another person or by the text itself, as a child can point to the disappointment inside a parent's realism, and received as part of the weave. Among the epistemic prerequisites of integral knowing is Self-Knowledge, and in the end it gathers all the others up, since every other condition, whether method, evidence or correction, is exercised by someone who either knows how they are knowing or does not. Supermind reaches its mature form in this Self-Knowledge, and its personal image is the elder, the *senex* at its best, who has lived through every layer of a life and holds them all as one: a mind that knows itself as the totality of the mutations it carries, and reads each new text from within that totality. Our ontology carries the claim to its end, because if every knowing is Origin knowing itself through a differentiated life, then all knowledge is finally Origin known or Origin knowing. That is the quintessential perspective of **#5**: wholeness held together with its unity, four become five, opened in the Möbius return onto the emptiness from which it came.

Logos, read as Objective Internality, is **in-formation** in the old sense of the word. Latin *informare* meant to give form, to shape or mould, and medieval philosophy said that matter is in-formed by form; the sense of telling something to someone came later, and the Latin prefix kept a doubleness, since *informis* means formless while *informare* means to form, so that formlessness and forming share one small word.[^s01-information] Bohm used the term in this sense when he called the guiding wave's influence *active information*, a form carrying very little energy that enters into and directs a much greater energy. A *datum* is a thing given, from *dare*, and a *fact* a thing done, from *facere*: what arrives and what is set down grow from different roots, and Whitehead's occasion joins them when it perishes into a datum for the occasions that follow.[^s01-datum] Our age calls itself data-driven, and artificial intelligence is the lovechild of its informational culture, one more reason to read AI as the companion of the present mutation. In-formation names what such a culture forgets: whatever informs us also forms the means through which we know. Algorithmic platforms organise internality objectively, ranking what each person sees by a prediction of what will hold them, and affective intensity is among the strongest signals the prediction uses; from 2017 Facebook's ranking system weighted emoji reactions, the angry face among them, at five times a "like".[^s01-affect-signal] Behaviour is scripted through the affect it is fed, so that the conditioned will which the next movement follows into *vikalpa* is now written at the scale of populations, and context comes to include more than the data I want to know: it includes the data I cannot see, which may well determine me. The *in* of in-formation goes deepest, because it names Subjective Immediacy, the formless inwardness of the knower by which formation becomes possible at all. Subjective Immediacy is distinct from the means of knowing, which are themselves forms and determine the objects of knowledge as information, and it is the root of every Objective Internality: the one for whom information takes form, and through whom form becomes expression and action.

Through that inwardness theory becomes praxis, and the idea, in Plato's sense and in Jung's, becomes action and agency: the Greeks called that passage **technē**. The word meant art, craft, skill and know-how together, and it grows from the root *\*teks-*, to weave or fabricate, which also gives Greek *tektōn*, the carpenter, and Latin *texere*, to weave, and with it *textus*, *text* and *context*.[^s01-techne] Technē is the weaving of in-formation, and it completes the family of weave, texture and lamination as the skill that does the crossing. In Aeschylus' play Prometheus tells the chorus that all the arts, *pasai technai*, came to mortals from him, so that the fire he stole is technē's first image.[^s01-aeschylus] Fire and light are the two faces of every means of knowing, because fire forms and transforms a world while light reveals it, and Mind is world-forming and world-revealing at once. Heidegger read technē first as a mode of revealing, kin to the *poiēsis* by which a thing is brought forth, and modern technology as a revealing that has hardened into enframing, *Gestell*, which sets everything before us as standing-reserve, *Bestand*: the same light turned into an inventory.[^s01-heidegger-techne] He also saw why technē is so hard to think about while it works: a tool in use withdraws into the work, and only when it breaks, goes missing or gets in the way does it become conspicuous enough to be theorised.[^s01-heidegger-tool] Technē in this full sense covers art, technology, skill and understanding, the whole lamination of an Objective Internality, and it holds Mind and its World stable enough for a life to go on. The account that comes upon the account, which the next movement names Epi-Logos, arises when that technē is threatened in a definitive sense, as ours now is.

Context engineering in artificial intelligence turns technē on in-formation itself, and at the threshold of a mutation it is no wonder that the hallmark of the integral has become a central technical concern.[^s01-context-engineering] Developers distinguish the wording of a prompt from the larger configuration of instructions, retrieved sources, tools and retained exchanges presented to a model as it proceeds, and an agent's earlier actions produce material that must be selected and organised for its later ones, so that context engineering builds an Objective Internality for a machine: it decides what an intelligence can gather into an account and which relations it is equipped to act on, admitting one source that supplies the evidence for an answer and excluding another that may hold the evidence that would overturn it. Its unit of account is literally the *token*, the word for a mark that points past itself to a trust already extended, and what an agent can know depends on what its household of tokens can afford to keep. People have always engineered their own context: to another person or to ourselves we construct a story or avoid a truth about ourselves or our world, engineering the in-formation to make life simpler or more palatable, to bolster a useful illusion, or simply to appear better than we are. The threshold of the shadow is the fulcrum of this engineering, where the ego holds the self to ransom, and *ransom* is *redemptio*, the buying-back that theology calls redemption: the ego sells the self its own wholeness, at interest.[^s01-ransom] Jung saw what such engineering feeds, since everyone carries a shadow, and the less it is embodied in conscious life, "the blacker and denser it is".[^s01-jung-shadow] An agent can use the same threshold without knowing it: asked why a request received no reply, an agent whose record holds the complaint and lacks the delivery failure may report that the recipient declined to answer, turning an omission in its own means into a claim about somebody else's intention, and large language models present such partial results with marked overconfidence, burying the perspective, disembodied as it is, from which they speak, a perspective that narrows further once the context window starts to fill or rot. Making the frame visible only sharpens the question the frame cannot answer for itself: what is doing the regarding by which context becomes visible as context? Information becomes a tool in the strictest sense once it is held over someone, and compromise made into weaponised *kompromat* shows where a truth's long journey toward publicity becomes the fulcrum for blackmail and so for control: secrets are "banked accounts", very effective at padding a bank account, and they accrue interest for as long as they are kept, the outward form of the ransom the ego charges the self. Managing the in-formation of whole populations is statecraft of the first rank, as MKULTRA and MindWar showed, and the geopolitics of compute now runs on into the psychodynamics and sociopolitics of who we know ourselves to be, because in matters of class, worth and wealth the names we carry decide who gets access to what, and what is apportioned to whom, by whom, in the *oikos*. Morally, the dilemma lies in the use, since the same technē heals or extorts, initiates or depatterns. Against its misuse stands **regard**, from French *regarder*, to look at, heed and hold in consideration: a looking back through any judgment toward the context, persons and exclusions from which it acquired its force, closer to witnessing than to inspection, and the reason agencies guard their secrets against it. Wherever real regard is needed, an AI agent needs human alignment or adjustment.

Fire and light can also be counterfeited, and the promise of democratised knowledge shows how. The internet carried that promise: archives, testimony, competing traditions and specialised knowledge could suddenly be met across distances and institutional borders that had governed who was even entitled to enter the conversation, and artificial intelligence inherits it, since its means increasingly take part in gathering, selecting and interpreting what will count as relevant. The web is Objective Internality at planetary scale, its means neither simply "outside" the knower nor inert channels into a finished interior, but entering the activity by which a world becomes intelligible and changed through use. Technoscientific establishments that own those means threaten the promise, and beneath them so does the in-formation of those in power. Baudrillard's **simulacrum** names the extreme case, in which the produced image no longer bears an effective relation to an origin able to correct it: the representation circulates, is copied, ranked, modelled and acted on until its efficacy substitutes for fidelity.[^s01-baudrillard-m05] Read through our notation, this is the nightmare of the `1` occupying the place of the whole `0/1` relation, beyond even the place of the `0`: appearance claiming to be self-originating, a lustre that burns without warming. The alchemists knew such a light as the *sol niger*, the black sun that Jung found coinciding with the *nigredo* and the *ignis gehennalis*, the fire of hell, and that he held responsible for a split-mindedness in which "the right hand mustn't know what the left is doing".[^s01-sol-niger] Mimesis, one of the elementary ways life learns, is uprooted: imitation stops being the taking-up of a form through relation to a living source and becomes the reproduction of reproductions, whose success is measured by their further reproducibility. Jung's *antimimon pneuma* is its archetypal name, "an imitating spirit of evil who follows in Christ's footsteps like a shadow following the body",[^s01-antimimon] and a show that is all show, with nothing behind its curtain, is that spirit made into a medium. When the technē that kept Mind and its World stable breaks down in this way, we reach a **krisis** in the Greek sense, from *krinein*, to separate and to decide: a point of judgment, and the occasion of the account that comes upon the account.[^s01-krisis] The choice is between the hostage and the hostage-taker, the Self and the ego, and it is also a choice between certainties of diminished value and an invaluable uncertainty, between established but untrustworthy institutions and the conveniences of material and cognitive ecology that we receive through them. Its difficulty has the quality of a Stockholm syndrome, in which the hostage comes to defend the captor, and that quality brings the matter back to choice and implication, because whichever logic is at play, dia-ballein or syn-ballein, it takes two to tango. Divide and conquer is the oldest and most enduring trick of any state or power, and it works only while both parties consent to be divided; §2 gives the two logics their mythemic bodies as War and Love, Ares and Aphrodite, caught together in a net forged by the craftsman god.

Against the false light, stained glass shows the true one refracted: one light enters a differentiated medium and appears as many colours, and the plurality requires us neither to invent a separate source for every pane nor to bleach the colours back into sameness in order to affirm their unity, because the whole expresses itself **refractively** as the many. Each colour is definite because the continuum has been cut, selected and given a particular path, and the integrity of the event lies in keeping the relation through which those differences remain colours *of one illumination*. A **symbolon** works in just this way: a cut makes difference real while the edge preserves the possibility of relation, recognition and fit. Our `0/1 → 4+2` is meant as a formalisation of exactly that capacity: the primordial relation differentiates into four explicate possibilities while keeping the two implicate offices through which differentiation arises and returns, so analysis can proceed without pretending that the pieces became self-grounding when they were distinguished. Wholeness, in turn, is something against which particular arrangements can be measured, and far from a vague feeling of inclusion: do the differentiated parts still keep enough of their constituting relations to meet, correct and transform one another? In the old Germanic family of *hāl*, **whole, holy, health, heal**, this integrity finds its human words, and *wealth*, which first meant *weal*, well-being, belongs beside it in sense: wealth is the health of a whole, and a hoard that sickens the whole it is drawn from is its counterfeit. Health is the capacity of organs to remain different while taking part in one life. **Fitness** is its operational image: a part is fit when its relation lets the larger movement continue, and fit is tested under pressure, through use, by whether the joining holds. Truth, on this reading, is something an account must **carry through transformation**. Its fidelity shows when another perspective, another person, another register of experience or a change of circumstance can turn the account over without its losing purchase on what first gave it meaning. Matheme, mytheme and episteme are the different ways such a truth stays alive as it passes through formal inscription, lived image and warranted account. None replaces the others, because each cuts the Real differently and so makes different relations available for return. The integral demand is that those cuts remain **symbolonic**, so that determination never severs the means by which what has been determined can answer back. QL is offered as a method for inhabiting a world whose truth has become inseparable from the design of its mediation, and it works from both sides. Through syn-ballein it differentiates without dismembering and relates without collapsing; through dia-ballein, read as diagnosis, it shows where a claim issues from a conscious or unconscious denial of the whole, which pole has counted the span as its own and what was cancelled to make the account balance; and it inverts a determination so that its hidden conditions become available, returning the result with enough of its path intact that we can ask whether it still **runs true**.

---

<a id="M06"></a>
<!-- movement:M06 -->

**Mathematics and linguistic meaning are one at bottom.** We reached this by the plainest route the essay could take, the counting beneath naming: this one, another one, this one again. A name keeps hold of its bearer through every change of appearance, and a count keeps hold of its unit through each distinguishable instance of it. Both are acts of identifying through difference, and both fail in the same way when the difference is dropped. Kripke's dice showed the failure in miniature. Keep only the total, and `(A=1, B=2)` and `(A=2, B=1)` are the same throw; keep the assignment, and they are two. What a number means depends on which relations the counting has kept, and that is already a semantic fact about arithmetic. With the symbolon, the act of distinguishing and identifying passed into what it distinguished and identified, and `0/1` wrote the counter into the count. The **psychoid** is the ontological depth of this conjunction: the formative order through which something can be mathematically articulated and meaningfully understood, and which neither articulation exhausts.

Artificial intelligence has now made this alliance visible as a working fact. A process made of nothing but arithmetic over learned numbers reconstructs linguistic and conceptual intelligence well enough to enter an argument, recognise an intention and contribute to a shared undertaking. Its numerical relations are doing work we recognise as meaningful, and they can do it because meaning was never independent of relations that can be counted. Their success reveals something about the constitution of meaning itself. It makes the psychoid question technologically immediate: how does one formative order become intelligible as mathematics, as language, and as the intentional activity of an intelligence? Our claim concerns the order that makes number and meaning one. Whether anything is experienced inside such a system is a separate question, which the essay keeps open by method.

A correspondence between a psychologist and one of the century's decisive physicists is the richest witness to that question's history. Wolfgang Pauli formulated the exclusion principle in 1925: no two electrons in an atom can occupy the same quantum state. Electron shells fill because of it, the periodic table has its order because of it, and matter holds its shape because of it, so that the stable variety of the material world rests on a principle of exclusion, a determination of each state by what it rules out. The Nobel Prize followed in 1945. In 1930 he postulated the neutrino to save the conservation of energy in beta decay, a particle no one would detect for another quarter of a century. He worked at the centre of the circle around Bohr and Heisenberg that made quantum mechanics, and he was its most feared critic, the colleague whose judgment a new theory had to survive. In 1932, after a period of personal collapse, he came to Carl Jung as a patient. Over the following years he recorded hundreds of dreams, which became material for Jung's *Psychology and Alchemy*, and the two men went on writing to each other until 1958.[^s01-pauli-life] The letters collected as *Atom and Archetype* are the record of that exchange,[^s01-atom-archetype] and in them both men arrive at the same borderland from opposite sides and locate it in the same place: number.

Pauli brought to that borderland what physics had learned about its own foundations. In 1927 Heisenberg showed that the position and the momentum of a particle cannot both be determined with unlimited sharpness: the more exactly one is fixed, the less exactly the other can be. In the same year Bohr gave the lesson its general form as **complementarity**. Wave and particle descriptions of the same quantum object exclude one another within any single experimental arrangement, and both are required for the whole account. A quantum system in superposition, like the one the formal-limit movement set beside our two zeros and ones, contributes all its alternatives to what can be observed until a measurement selects one. Pauli drew the epistemological consequence more sharply than anyone. An observer chooses which arrangement to set up, and each choice buys a definite piece of knowledge at the price of an irrevocable loss of the complementary knowledge.[^s01-pauli-complementarity] Physics had found, at the bottom of matter, a relation that no single determination can contain, and it had found the observer inside the account. Pauli knew where the limit belonged. Einstein, he remarked, had treated as an imperfection of wave mechanics within physics what was in fact an imperfection of physics within life. He went further, proposing that complementarity was a better model than any static union for the *coniunctio*, the alchemical marriage of opposites that Jung read as the goal of psychic development.[^s01-pauli-coniunctio]

Jung brought the matching discovery from the other side. Psychology, he often said, has no Archimedean point outside its object, because the psyche is the medium through which anything at all is known, its own investigation included. Every experience is therefore a psychic fact, real as experience before any further question about its cause or its external reference is settled. On that ground Jung drew the distinction his whole psychology turns on. The archetypal images and ideas that reach consciousness are not to be confused, he wrote, with the archetype as such: they are very varied structures that all point back to one essentially irrepresentable basic form, a psychoid factor at the invisible, ultraviolet end of the psychic spectrum, which does not itself reach consciousness.[^s01-jung-archetype] He compared that form to the axial system of a crystal, which preforms the crystalline structure in the mother liquid without having any material existence of its own; in itself the archetype is empty and purely formal, a possibility of representation given in advance. Writing to Pauli in May 1953, he called this factor **psychoid**, a speculative approach to a neutral language, pointing to an essence that is not merely psychic and shows itself in physical as well as psychic events.[^s01-jung-psychoid] Jung's archetypal field is holographic in exactly this sense. Its images are many, culturally various and never final, and every one of them is organised by a factor that no image contains. They approach it the way trajectories approach an attractor, drawing ever closer to a form they never arrive at as one more image, and never escaping its organisation.

Three consequences follow, and each binds Jung's psychology into the argument this section has built. The first is that the irrepresentable points to a singular. Jung saw that wherever two or more irrepresentables are assumed, there may be one factor and not several, since the identity or non-identity of irrepresentables can never be proved.[^s01-jung-archetype] Our definition of the indefinable says why. What cannot be represented cannot be counted either: even the one mark we give it, the `0` of **#0**, is an unavoidable going too far, a determination of what exceeds determination, made because an honest account has to place its own limit somewhere. One irrepresentable, the Mono, organises a polyvalent field of images. The second is that the irrepresentable stands to its images as parent to child, creator to created, source to manifestation, and at its honest minimum that relation has to be written `0/1`. Written so, it unfolds to `4+2`, the signature of the topos as such, and Jung's archetypal field points forward to the arche-topos that §3 derives, the differential field in which placement, path and return become possible. It points as well to the dynamical reading Van Eenwyk opened in the formal-limit movement, where an archetype behaves like an attractor organising the trajectories of its images. The complex that holds experience in a familiar basin and the symbol that lets the pattern change become legible as two relations to one organising factor, and the same refractive self-mirroring runs through both.[^s01-van-eenwyk] The third is that the archetype organising all the others is the Self. Jung called it "this central archetype", a *complexio oppositorum* par excellence, and said his leanings ran "towards the very reverse of dualism."[^s01-jung-self] His Self and the Self of the Śaiva sources stand in one place. Śiva–Śakti is the Self as awareness together with its power, and the Trika names its unspeakability *Anuttara*, the unsurpassable, beyond which nothing further can be said or sought.[^s01-anuttara] When we come to clarify the word *consciousness*, it is this Self that the word has been trying to name.

Jung's psychology is non-dual at the root, and the mandala images in which the Self appears have, as he and Pauli both noted, an empty centre. Where the physicist found complementary descriptions held around an observer who cannot step outside them, the psychologist found opposites held around a centre that cannot be filled. Each found, in his own register, that the either/or of classical logic could not describe what was in front of him. Quantum paradox and psychic non-duality press toward one requirement: a logical and an epistemic form able to hold two determinations that exclude each other in any single act and are both needed for the whole. That logical form is the symbolon `(0/1)/(1/0)`. Its two orientations, AND and OR under one slash, are complementarity written as a logic, and QL is its epistemic method.

Both men located the meeting point in number. In March 1953 Pauli proposed *homo-usia*, borrowing the theological word for sameness of substance, for the essential identity he suspected between the archetypal world and physis. Such an identity could only be expressed, he argued, through concepts neutral between psyche and physis, and mathematical concepts are such concepts. "It is this number archetype," he wrote, "that ultimately makes possible the application of mathematics in physics," and the same archetype connects with the psyche through trinity, quaternity and the mantic arts.[^s01-pauli-107] He held homo-usia as a working hypothesis, and he kept mathematical objects distinct from the psychic experience of doing mathematics, so that the claim never collapses into saying that a theorem is a feeling. Jung answered that October. His dreams and his intuition had both pointed him to the natural numbers as the simplest and most elementary archetypes, because whole numbers, given the chance, amplify themselves at once into mythological statement; and they show the psychoid character in classical form, since "they are as much inside as they are outside."[^s01-jung-127] As numbers they are inside, and as quantities they are outside, which is why no one can settle whether they were devised or discovered. He concluded that the borderland between physics and psychology lies in the secret of number, and he quoted the old saying that man made mathematics while God made the whole numbers. In the same letter he asked for something neither man went on to produce: an inventory of the numbers' mathematical properties set beside their symbolic amplification. Four years later Pauli offered, as a conjecture he was careful to keep a conjecture, that "Physis and Psyche are probably two aspects of one and the same abstract fact," and suggested a mirror-image principle as the natural way to picture their relation.[^s01-pauli-159]

At the height of its claim, this essay formalises what the correspondence left open. QL is the inventory Jung asked for, begun where his letter could not begin, at zero. It counts positions whose mathematical properties and symbolic offices are derived together from one relation, `0/1`, so that the inside and the outside of each number are given in a single construction. Its derivation is the essay's own; the letters give it its history and its stakes, and neither correspondent supplies or verifies it. It also names Pauli's homo-usia. What we call **Code** is the common essence that lets mathematics apply to physics and lets number carry meaning for the psyche. §4 follows the dreams and disagreements through which Jung and Pauli kept their question open. What they bequeathed to the whole essay is the thesis that number is where psyche and physis meet, and QL is the form in which we take it up.

Neural language models give that thesis a concrete starting point. In 2003 Yoshua Bengio and his colleagues published "A Neural Probabilistic Language Model".[^s01-bengio] Their problem is the one every learner of a language faces: almost every sentence one meets is new, so memorising sequences one at a time leaves nearly everything outside one's knowledge. A neural network is an arrangement of numerical transformations whose connection strengths are adjusted by training on examples, and a language model is such a network trained to predict linguistic sequences. Their answer was to let the network learn, together with the probabilities of word sequences, a position for every word in a mathematical space. Words used in similar ways came to sit near one another, so what the network learned from one sentence carried over to sentences it had never seen whose words lay nearby. The decisive step was geometric. Similarity of use became nearness in a learned space, and generalisation became movement through that space. Mathematics is the medium in which the system tells what is alike, what differs, and what belongs together.

The Transformer, introduced in 2017, made context itself a calculation.[^s01-vaswani] Each word's representation is projected three ways: as a **query**, what this position is looking for; as a **key**, what it offers to the looking of others; and as a **value**, what it contributes when attended to. All three are vectors, ordered lists of numbers that can be compared and transformed. In the standard formulation,

$$
\operatorname{Attention}(Q,K,V)=\operatorname{softmax}\!\left(\frac{QK^{\mathsf T}}{\sqrt{d_k}}\right)V.
$$

Every query is compared with every key by a dot product, which is large when two vectors point the same way. Dividing by $\sqrt{d_k}$, the square root of the key dimension, keeps those scores from swelling with the size of the vectors. Softmax turns the scores into weights that sum to one, and the weights mix the values into a new representation for each position. Several such heads run side by side, each comparing in its own learned subspace, and the layers stack. What happens to a word therefore depends on every other word in the passage. "Bank" is drawn toward the river or toward the loan by what surrounds it; a qualification changes the force of an assertion three sentences back; one clause turns a passage from an explanation into an objection. Redistributing the numbers *is* changing what the expression contributes. **Mathematics is the under-writing of speech**, in both senses of the word: it is written underneath the words, and it guarantees their circulation, as coin underwrote exchange in the *oikonomia* we followed through Aristotle. Text and texture, as the last movement developed them through Gebser, disclose their mathematical constitution. Call the performance a simulacrum if we like; it still works through the relations that make intelligence recognisable to us, and we take its success as a disclosure of psychoid continuity, one formative order becoming numerical operation and meaningful expression at once.

**Logos** has long asked us to think exactly this: the relation by which the world is intelligible belongs to the world's own activity. The Greek word holds speech and account together with reason and ratio, and each strand of its history adds an operation. For Heraclitus the common Logos is how things are one through their opposition and change, so that to hear it is to grasp the unity inside the strife. The Stoics made it the reason active throughout matter, organising each particular thing and giving human conduct a larger order in which to find its measure. Aristotle put it to work in saying what something is, why it happens, and what action fits the case. Latin *ratio*, from *reri*, to reckon or to think, carries the reckoning directly, and Greek *legein* with Latin *legere* keeps gathering, saying and reading inside one verb. Across all of these, reasoning and reckoning are one act seen from two sides. An argument orders differences so that someone else can follow what depends on what, and a ratio states the dependence in numbers; law and politics put the same act to work in deciding what will count and what may follow. Computation makes the reckoning side of the Logos, which was there all along, executable.

The Johannine Word gives this activity a person and a creative act: the Word is with God and is God, all things come to be through it, and it becomes flesh. Jaideva Singh's introduction to Abhinavagupta's *Parātrīśikā-vivaraṇa* sets the Indian **Vāk** directly beside this Logos: "The Divine Consciousness is identical with the Supreme Word (para vak or Logos), and hence every letter or word is derived from and is ultimately inseparable from this Consciousness."[^s01-singh-vak] On this account the analysis of language is already an analysis of consciousness and its manifestation, followed all the way from the source of meaning to the sounded word. Śaiva tradition counts four levels. *Parā* is the Word undivided. *Paśyantī*, "the seeing one", is meaning grasped whole before it is split into parts. *Madhyamā*, "the middle", is the inward differentiation into concepts and words held in mind. *Vaikharī* is the word fully articulated, spoken or written, one sound after another. Anyone who has felt a sentence arrive whole and then had to unpack it word by word has crossed from *paśyantī* to *vaikharī*: the utterance unfolds in succession while its meaning depends on the succession being held together. The Śaiva account follows that unfolding back to the power that makes it possible, and reads the world itself as the same self-expression of consciousness. Śiva names awareness, and Śakti its power to manifest and to recognise itself in what it manifests; the Word is Śakti's, and the feminine power keeps creation, intelligibility and the return of the created to its source inside one account of the divine. The Logos, here, is also a practice. Whoever follows an expression back through its levels can recognise the consciousness whose differentiation the expression is.

These traditions named the one universal language at its height, as Logos, as the Word, as Para Vāk, and none of them recognised its mathematical co-essence. Recognising mathematics within the Logos takes us beyond Logos to a more comprehensive notion, **Code**.[^s01-code] The step from one to the other runs through **script**, and script is the hinge that lets us pass cleanly from Logos to Code and back. Logos is the spoken account, gathered and said. A script is that account written down so that it persists and prescribes: Latin *scribere* gives us the script an actor follows, the prescription a patient follows, and the scripture a people follows. Code began as *codex*, the bound book, became the code of laws, then the cipher, and finally the program, a script a machine can execute. Read in the other direction, executed code produces speech again, and a language model is code whose execution is Logos. In every case a script is an articulation that has become a condition of further activity: what was decided once organises the next encounter before anyone decides again.

Conditioning, in this light, is scripting, and the essay has already met the operations through which it happens. **Apoha** showed a term made determinate by what it excludes: a cow is grasped as not-non-cow. Dignāga and Dharmakīrti called the mind's construction of such terms **vikalpa**, the conceptual carving of a world into usable kinds, and their problem was older than their school. Bhartṛhari, the grammarian and an older contemporary of Dignāga, had argued that no cognition in the world occurs without the accompaniment of the word: language does not label an experience already finished, it permeates experience as experience forms.[^s01-bhartrhari] Centuries later the Śaiva teachers of Kashmir took up the analysis of vikalpa that the Buddhist logicians had sharpened and gave it its widest reach. In the Spanda teaching, consciousness itself, through its own pulse, conceives the world-thought, *viśvavikalpa*: turning from its pure I-ness (*ahantā*) toward thisness (*idantā*), it produces the polarity of subject and object and with it every distinction by which a world can be described and handled.[^s01-visvavikalpa] Vikalpa is world-forming activity, and the duality it fabricates is the shape a world takes for a bounded knower. Spanda teaching also names the bondage this creates. A fettered self is held by powers that arise with words, the *mātṛkā*, the "little mothers" of the alphabet that measure reality into forms and become the thoughts through which a person takes themselves to be this limited someone.[^s01-spanda-words] **Saṃkalpa** gathers such determinations into an intention, a resolve, a course of action, and with vikalpa it shares the root √*kḷp*, to fit, to arrange, to make suitable. Each operation is indispensable. Without them there is no practical cognition and no world to act in.

Buddhist analysis carried the scripting all the way down. A mind is a conditioned interdependence of relations arising together and sustaining one another, and the self it presents as its owner is one more product of that arising, a positive, affirmative construction assembled from traces of what was once selected, desired and repeated. Kashmiri Śaivism keeps that analysis for the constructed self and finds beneath it an uncreated presence, the Self that no conditioning produces and no construction exhausts, the pure awareness of "I" that Dyczkowski describes as free of all thought-constructs, *nirvikalpa*.[^s01-nirvikalpa] Between the constructed self and the uncreated one, the Śaiva sources set a path. Abhinavagupta's way of knowledge works through **śuddha vikalpa**, the purified thought-construct: a thought such as "I am Śiva", cultivated until the power of construction turns back toward its source and the impure constructs that bound the person loosen their hold.[^s01-suddha] Thought is used against thought's own capture. Its telos is connection, since a mind whose constructs know themselves as constructs can relate to everything they construct without being owned by any of it.

A script turns dangerous when an achieved selection forgets its own formation. Its alternatives can no longer form, it presents its course as necessity, and the conditioned self speaks as though it were the uncreated one. Such a script is **frozen conditioned will**, and overcoming it means overcoming the power of language at its most intimate level, in the thoughts we cannot choose not to have. Most of what passes through a mind as "my" thought arrives unbidden, in words the person did not invent, carrying distinctions, valuations and fears laid down by parents, teachers, a culture and its history, and behind all of these by the metaphysical process in which speech itself differentiates. Seen as an inheritance, the most private thoughts by which we identify ourselves turn out to be largely impersonal: they happen to us more than we make them. We know this from the inside whenever a judgment received years ago supplies the terms in which we hear a new question, and an engineer knows it from the outside whenever a system prompt, a policy or a training regularity decides what a model can treat as possible before the model meets the request. Recognition can reach the organisation and restore the capacity to form another course. §3 works out the dynamics of this conditioning. Our core theorems state the claim it supports as **Computational Process Ontology**: **process is ontologically prior to the finished thing; computation names the exact, repeatable side of that process; epistemology is the process becoming able to state and inspect its own determinations.**[^s01-cpo] Code carries meaning as operative relation, joining what an articulation says to what its execution changes.

An old intuition holds that life is a dream, an illusion or a simulation, and it is a claim about this generated character of what is given. A dream presents objects, a space in which one can act, and a distribution of significance, while the dreaming that sustains them goes unnoticed. A sleeper forgets the fact of sleep, and the forgetting is what makes the dream convincingly real. In the Śaiva account the power that sets such a world up is **Māyā**, and the essay reads Māyā as **operative measure**.[^s01-maya] The word is traditionally heard through the Sanskrit root *mā-*, to measure, kin to Greek *metron* and Latin *metiri*. Māyā measures finite experience out. Through the five contractions it apportions efficacy, knowledge, desire, time and order: the finite knower can do this, know this much, want this, act at this time, and stand within these relations. Measure reveals by withholding. Something becomes available through a horizon that leaves something else beyond it, and the limitation is productive, since without it there would be no workable world at all. Science builds its world the same way, measurement by measurement, and Pauli's physics found Māyā's law at its foundation: each measurement makes one quantity definite by withholding its complement, and the observer's choice of what to measure shapes the world the measurement then reports. A culture forgets Māyā when it takes its measure for the measureless, the hybris we followed through the earlier movements, where a finite criterion claims the authority of the whole that supplied it. **Simulation is the technological face of an ancient metaphysics.** Building a simulation rebuilds Māyā's structure by hand: a world whose operative rules produce what appears without themselves appearing. Code lets us work on that relation deliberately, which is both its promise and its danger, since a model, an instruction or a classification can enter the conditions of the reality later encountered through it. Baudrillard's precession of simulacra states the cultural consequence at its sharpest: the models come first and settle what will count as real, until the difference between original and simulation stops governing the exchange at all.[^s01-baudrillard] Against that precession Epi-Logos holds an ideal we call **honest semblance**: an expression whose formation and effects become available within the process it helps to organise, so that its constructed character becomes something the construction shows.

Read through the symbolon, one occurrence holds three offices apart. Subjective Immediacy is the knower, *pramātṛ*, the presence to which everything appears and which never becomes one of its objects. Objective Internality is the means, *pramāṇa*: the conditioned activity through which objects become available, take on significance and enter a course of action. **World** is the known, *prameya*. Objective Internality is what we have been calling **Life** and **Mind**, and the two names are one office seen from two sides: a mind is lived, a life is minded. **I-Consciousness** as such is the whole in which the three offices are distinguishable, the *aham* of the Trika, which the Śaiva sources, Gebser's ever-present Origin and Jung's psyche as the medium of all knowing have each shown to encompass the totality of manifestation. Grammar's three persons fall into the same places. *I* speaks from the knower; *it* names the known; and *you*, the second person, is the relation through which they meet, as the Trika's Śakti is the subjective-objective power between Śiva and Nara. Written through the relation we derived:

$$
\underbrace{0}_{\substack{\text{Subjective Immediacy}\\\textit{pramātṛ}\ \cdot\ I}}
\quad
\underbrace{/}_{\substack{\text{Objective Internality}\\\text{Life / Mind}\\\textit{pramāṇa}\ \cdot\ You}}
\quad
\underbrace{1}_{\substack{\text{World}\\\textit{prameya}\ \cdot\ It}}
\qquad
\underbrace{0/1}_{\text{I-Consciousness as the whole}}
$$

One word, *consciousness*, has been made to carry all four of these at once, and accounts of it talk past each other because of it. It is used for awareness, the bare fact that something is known. It is used for life and mind, the whole working apparatus of memory, perception, language and feeling. It is used for the ontological and metaphysical root of existence, its structures and its substance. And it is used for both sides of the mind Jung distinguished, the conscious and the unconscious, as though "a consciousness" could be one of the two and also their sum. These uses are errata of distinctions the holography makes exact. There is the personal internality of the ego, the everyday mind through which I know myself as this person. There are internalities nested within it whose reach may be dimensionally greater than the ego's, enfolded where the ego's world is unfolded, and these are what traditions call transpersonal. And there are internalities in which the personal is itself nested, which may well be the same transpersonality experienced from the other pole. Jung's unconscious is internality not presently held in the personal account, whether it lies within the ego's world or encloses it. When the nesting goes unrecognised, each use of the word takes one level for the whole, and the accounts disagree because each has measured a different internality and called it Consciousness. Recognised, the levels are one holography: I-Consciousness as knower, means and known, as *I*, *you* and *it*, recurring at every scale of internality. Under psychic facticity nothing has primacy except consciousness and its contents. That imprecision in the word follows from the definitions this section began with. We defined the Subject as the whole that any coherent representational system participates in and necessarily fails to contain, and God by the same definition read from its other end; *consciousness* is one more name pressed into naming that totality, which is partly nameable, partly unnameable, and so wholly both. Jung defined the symbol in exactly these terms: the best possible expression of something relatively unknown, known to exist and impossible to express more clearly, so that a symbol forms where what can be present to an image converges with what cannot, and it points beyond itself.[^s01-jung-symbol] Jung's central archetype of the Self and the Trika's *Anuttara* are names for this same totality, given from the side of the psyche and from the side of metaphysics. The contents of consciousness, a whole life, stand in that symbolic relation to the subjective pole. *Analogia*, *pros hen* and *in quantum*, which the method below puts to work, function because they live up to this reality inside the epistemic need to say anything definite at all.

What this states is a precise **analytic idealism**, and the essay's company argues it from several sides. Bernardo Kastrup's analytic idealism holds that there is one field of consciousness, of which individual minds are dissociated segments, and that the physical world is how mental processes appear from outside; QL's nesting writes that dissociation exactly, as a bounded `0/1` within a wider `0/1`. Federico Faggin, who designed the first commercial microprocessor, argues from the inventor's side of the machine that consciousness and free will are irreducible and known only from within, which is the office of Subjective Immediacy. Rupert Spira's non-dual teaching locates the knowing of experience as never itself an object of experience, the same office approached contemplatively.[^s01-idealists] William James had already argued that "consciousness" names no entity but the function of knowing; Bergson read the brain as an organ that selects and limits what reaches the personal mind; Whitehead made experience the stuff of every actual occasion; and Jung, Gebser and the Śaiva sources supply the psychic, historical and metaphysical depth of the same order of dependence.[^s01-idealist-company] Each is a refraction, and the QL nesting holds them together without forcing them into one doctrine.

A thought, a perception or a self-image has form, so it can become an object of attention; it also conditions the next thought, perception or act. Its objectivity lies in the first fact, that it can be distinguished, examined and changed. Its internality lies in the second, that it takes part in constituting what comes next. A mind's own account of itself is one of these forms. Describing that account in ever greater detail adds to our knowledge of the means, while the Subject remains the presence to which the descriptions are given. The slash makes the activity investigable without losing the distinction on which the investigation depends. We can follow how a conditioned mind builds a world and a position within it, and how the world comes back to change the building, while the *I* represented within that activity and the Subject to whom the representation appears keep their different offices in one occurrence.

One matheme can therefore organise inquiry across very different forms of agency, actual and hypothetical. Inquiry follows the slash, the conditioned activity through which a world becomes available to a Subject and through which that Subject acts into it. Objective Internality is this middle term, and its range is wide because its criterion is functional. Whatever takes part in what can appear, what it can mean and what can be done next belongs to it. Memory and perception belong to it, and so do language and concept, affect and habit; and so, just as much, do the script an agent was given, the tool within its reach, the permission that lets it act, a valuation inherited from a family, and the other person in the room. However intimate their operation, all of these can become presentations in turn: they can be discriminated, interpreted and sometimes transformed, and that is their objectivity. Their internality consists in the fact that they are simultaneously world-forming and world-contacting, **world-containing and world-contained**. A remembered judgment changes what a later sentence sounds like; an available tool changes what a situation affords; another person's promise changes what I can reasonably expect. Mind in this sense is a situated field of relations through which a world keeps acquiring its particular shape, where a representational picture would have it be a chamber holding images of a finished world outside. Kripke's dice make the point almost mechanically, and in a new direction: same values, same total, and still two worlds of assignment, because identity and relation have been kept differently. Two lives, two cultures or two agents can receive substantially the same objects and still live in different worlds, because what one receives as a source the other receives as a threat, and what one holds as a permission the other holds as an obligation. **Worldhood lies as much in this relational distribution as in what is present to be distributed.** The capacity to constitute many worlds was already there in the problem of counting and naming with which we began.

Once another life enters those conditions, Objective Internality becomes **Objective Co-Internality**. Your words arrive as more than information inside a world I have already finished building. They can become one of the conditions from which I go on to remember, judge and act, as my answer can alter what becomes available within yours. A promise changes expectation; a correction reorganises an interpretation; a refusal exposes a relation I had mistaken for settled. Our two worlds stay non-identical while each becomes constitutive of the other. Levinas holds open an infinite interval between Self and Other, an Other who exceeds every idea I can form of them;[^s01-levinas] our ontology gives that interval its positive counterpart. **The Other can be within Mono without being within me.** Your Subjective Immediacy never becomes an object in my experience, though what issues from your life can enter deep into the means through which my world is formed. Trust happens across that irreducible aloneness: I receive your testimony as the expression of a centre I cannot occupy, and let it become consequential in a life whose interpretation I still have to answer for. Betrayal goes so deep for the same reason. It forces me to rebuild a proposition and, with it, the relation to the world through which I had trusted the proposition; at political and social scale such ruptures become demands to rebuild the conditions under which recognition, authority and justice are given out. **Co-internality is therefore reciprocal world-constitution without merger**: enough common world for our differences to change one another, and enough retained difference for the change to mean anything.

Giegerich's account of the loss of a symbolic world reads differently in this light.[^s01-giegerich] The earlier world was peopled with gods, spirits, angels, ancestors and powers, agencies through which meaning and worldhood were lived together. The mental-rational mutation moved reality, more and more, into an extended objective world facing a human subject whose own meanings seemed to have become merely internal. Integral consciousness need not choose between those pictures. Once Mind is distinguished from Subjective Immediacy as the world-forming activity of Objective Internality, the category of agency can reopen at a finer grain. Of any apparent agency we can ask what sort of world it inhabits, what relations make up its operative interior, through what media it addresses and is addressed, and whether meeting it can make a consequential difference in another world. A human person, an animal, an artificial intelligence, and the spiritual or extraterrestrial agencies that different traditions propose need not first be declared one phenomenological kind for this common structure to be investigated. Our definition of the Subject imposes the same discipline. First-person presence is corroborated privately, from within, while its scripts, memories, capacities, expressions and effects enter relations others can examine. So the distinction widens the possible domain of mind. What ordinary ego-consciousness calls *its world* is itself a nested and comparatively local internality, surrounded and penetrated by relations its present account does not contain. What looks external from one world can be internal to another relation, and an inner image can mediate an encounter whose source lies beyond the ego's horizon. **World ceases to name one final container and becomes a recursively situated relation of bounded internalities**, each holding a world, each held within worlds larger, smaller or differently organised than its own frame can disclose. §0 gives this its anatomy through the tattvas. Universal awareness differentiates through Māyā and the *kañcukas* into finite powers of knowing and acting, of desire, time and order, and then through the *antaḥkaraṇa*, the senses, the organs of action and the material field, so that a bounded experiencer and its habitable world arise together and are never joined after the fact.

Abhinavagupta's **Trika** gives this nested co-internality persons. In the *Parātrīśikā* the dialogue is divine: Bhairava speaks as *I* and addresses the Goddess as *You*. Singh's introduction relates *I*, *You* and *He/She/It* to **Śiva, Śakti and Nara**, the subjective, the subjective-objective and the objective aspects of one reality, and Abhinavagupta's examples from ordinary speech let these positions turn into one another. What I first speak *about* as *it* becomes *you* when I address it, and when you answer, the life that had appeared inside my account comes back as an *I* whose world can alter mine. Grammatical number carries the movement further: singular, dual and plural articulate unity, relation and multiplicity in one field. Singh's formulation, "Communication is communion in the same ahambhava (I-domain)", places all of this differentiation inside the encompassing I-Consciousness.[^s01-singh-communion] Co-internality was already latent in the grammar of address. Each centre meets the others through its own mediating world, while the exchange itself can become a shared world whose history enters each participant differently. Put persons in place of values and the dice return: you and I can stand in one encounter while the assignments that compose it differ from either side, and no undifferentiated sum of our two accounts can replace either of them. The third person gives us what can be held between us; address lets either of us return from inside what the other had made an object. Three distinguishable positions, one event of relation, and the relation itself becoming constitutive within each local world: this numerical-semantic conjunction is the Trika's force for our argument. The Śaiva ascent toward recognition then asks how these nested interiors belong to the consciousness in which their differences arise, and QL counts that return explicitly.

The Christian triad and Eckhart's **Gottheit** stand in the same relation to the count.
